35: 
36: All selected file links responded successfully to a bounded HTTPS range request and returned TIFF headers. File sizes, modification times and the SHA-256 hashes of the first 4,096 bytes were recorded. Original full-file checksums are not published in the retrieved records; the downloader records full-file SHA-256 after transfer, checks source identity/size/range behaviour, and requires an OpenSlide decode check. This is an acquisition integrity audit, not independent validation of every source pixel.
37: 
38: ## Matching and exclusions
39: 
44: Tumor status comes from the archived ESAC specimen records preserved by the IDC conversion project. Full stripped slide labels are matched exactly when available. Abbreviated identifiers such as `[fe]-c3b8ed_D1_D1` are matched only when their first two and last six hexadecimal characters, section suffix and patient agree with exactly one image and one specimen. Abbreviated links are separately identified, allowing a strict-link sensitivity analysis. Repeated nested lists of all slides in a case are not accepted as proof of the individual specimen identity.
45: 
46: Images without a molecular record, without a unique specimen link, or without documented tumor-tissue status are excluded. Exclusion counts: {json.dumps(s['excluded_counts'])}. `excluded_images.csv` retains all reasons. Selection uses metadata and acquisition integrity, never model predictions or endpoint performance. All qualifying slides are retained for the future hierarchical pooling protocol; no best-performing slide is selected.
47: 
48: Linkage is at patient level. The image has not been proven to be from the exact block used for molecular assays. The archived specimen records also do not reliably identify frozen versus FFPE preparation, so equivalence to the TCGA diagnostic preparation is not established. These limitations must be retained in the external-validation report. The current stage documents tumor annotations and decode readiness; visual quality review and image-based exclusions are still pending.
49: 
54: The counts are before visual image QC and are not final evaluated sample sizes. Binary labels are 0 = negative and 1 = positive. PAM50 uses the original TCGA model order: 0 = LumA, 1 = LumB, 2 = Basal, 3 = Her2. Normal-like and missing PAM50 are excluded. Missing and equivocal HER2 are excluded; gene amplification, proteomic status and PAM50 are not substituted for clinical HER2.
55: 
56: PIK3CA positivity requires a reported mutation in the original TCGA qualifying variant-class set. The cBioPortal sequenced list, sample `sequenced` flags and WXS profile matrix agree on all 122 samples. A negative reference means no qualifying reported mutation in this profiled denominator, not guaranteed base-level callability or clinically confirmed wild type. The original TCGA class set was checked directly against the supplied v2 labeling script. Source somatic filtering differs across cohorts and remains a limitation.
57: 
58: The endpoint definitions are frozen before external feature extraction or prediction. The future TCGA-trained models and calibrators must also be frozen before inspecting external outcomes. No external-outcome tuning, model selection or recalibration is allowed in the primary analysis.
59: 
64: ## Run the pilot
65: 
66: Use `Start-BRCAExternalStage1.ps1 -Mode Pilot` with your existing BioMedical project and `D:\\CPTAC_BRCA_WSI` as the new image directory. The script uses the existing `.venv-brca-revision` environment to decode the downloaded slides. No new Hugging Face login is needed for this acquisition stage.
67: 
68: {pilot_table}
69: 
70: The pilot selects one slide from each of the first three patient identifiers, without inspecting labels. It verifies downloading, TIFF/WSI decoding, dimensions and physical pixel spacing, and generates thumbnails for review. It does not estimate performance. Partial files can be resumed by rerunning the same command. Existing completed files are checked against their receipts rather than overwritten.
71: 
72: At completion, upload the report ZIP printed by the script. It includes acquisition receipts, decode checks and thumbnails, but no full slide images or credentials. If a transfer is interrupted, the script preserves partial files and writes an interruption report where possible.
73: 
74: `-Mode Audit` only checks package integrity and required disk space for the pilot. `-Mode DownloadAll` downloads the full locked inventory; it is provided for the later acquisition step. Start with the pilot so its decoder checks and thumbnails can be reviewed before the full transfer. The original TCGA images, features and results are not changed.
75: 
76: ## Subsequent work
77: 
78: After a successful pilot: review slide quality; complete acquisition; freeze an outcome-independent patch/QC protocol consistent with the TCGA protocol; extract UNI and Hibou-B on the same external patches; finalize and checksum the TCGA-trained prediction objects; and evaluate each endpoint once with patient-level uncertainty. Same-patient links, abbreviated-specimen sensitivity, source assay differences and pretraining uncertainty must accompany the final results. No external accuracy or AUROC is available at stage 1.
79:

FILE: BRCA_ExternalValidation_Stage1\README.md
Line numbers refer to the displayed text (JSON may be reformatted).
20: The existing BRCA environment must be available at `.venv-brca-revision`. The downloader uses Python's standard library; slide checks use the already installed OpenSlide and Pillow. No package installation or model login is performed.
21: 
22: For integrity and space checks alone, use `-Mode Audit`. Full acquisition is available with `-Mode DownloadAll` after the pilot is reviewed. Detailed source and reproducibility instructions are in `REPRODUCIBILITY.md`.

FILE: BRCA_ExternalValidation_Stage1\REPRODUCIBILITY.md
Line numbers refer to the displayed text (JSON may be reformatted).
5: `code/prepare_inventory.py` uses the cohort-builder subset, the archived CPTAC2/BR specimen records and the cBioPortal sample/clinical records. It saves the current per-image JSON and bounded remote file probes, verifies patient and image identities, and creates the download manifest. Exact and uniquely abbreviated specimen links are distinguished. The public export contains a pair of swapped node identifiers; the code resolves this only through a unique already observed record with the exact patient and image, recording both old and new node identifiers.
6: 
7: The original 193 MB cohort-builder file contains unrelated collections and is not duplicated in this package. Its hash and extraction rule are recorded in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.json`; the exact CPTAC-BRCA subset is supplied. The original specimen ZIP hash and the extracted member name are also recorded. `code/refresh_sources.py` can fetch a new raw snapshot into a separate directory. It contacts public sources and does not require a login. A refreshed snapshot must be reviewed as a new version rather than replacing the frozen acquisition silently.
8: 
9: To rebuild the existing linkage from the supplied source subset, with Python 3.11+:
10:

FILE: BRCA_ExternalValidation_Stage1\STAGE1_REPORT.md
Line numbers refer to the displayed text (JSON may be reformatted).
3: The image/specimen linkage and remote file preflight are complete. This package starts a technical download pilot; it does not compute external predictions or performance.
4: 
5: ## Acquisition result
6: 
7: - Molecular study: cBioPortal `brca_cptac_2020`, 122 patients / primary-tumor samples.
8: - Patients with matching image identifiers: 120.
9: - Ready patient-level tumor-image links: 120 patients, 391 slides, 67.91 GB (63.25 GiB).
10: - Specimen links: {"exact": 298, "unique_abbreviated_specimen_alias": 93}.
12: - Technical pilot: 3 slides, 514.5 MB.
13: 
14: All selected file links responded successfully to a bounded HTTPS range request and returned TIFF headers. File sizes, modification times and the SHA-256 hashes of the first 4,096 bytes were recorded. Original full-file checksums are not published in the retrieved records; the downloader records full-file SHA-256 after transfer, checks source identity/size/range behaviour, and requires an OpenSlide decode check. This is an acquisition integrity audit, not independent validation of every source pixel.
15: 
16: ## Matching and exclusions
17: 
22: Tumor status comes from the archived ESAC specimen records preserved by the IDC conversion project. Full stripped slide labels are matched exactly when available. Abbreviated identifiers such as `[fe]-c3b8ed_D1_D1` are matched only when their first two and last six hexadecimal characters, section suffix and patient agree with exactly one image and one specimen. Abbreviated links are separately identified, allowing a strict-link sensitivity analysis. Repeated nested lists of all slides in a case are not accepted as proof of the individual specimen identity.
23: 
24: Images without a molecular record, without a unique specimen link, or without documented tumor-tissue status are excluded. Exclusion counts: {"NO_MATCHED_MOLECULAR_RECORD": 259, "NO_UNIQUE_SPECIMEN_LINK": 4}. `excluded_images.csv` retains all reasons. Selection uses metadata and acquisition integrity, never model predictions or endpoint performance. All qualifying slides are retained for the future hierarchical pooling protocol; no best-performing slide is selected.
25: 
26: Linkage is at patient level. The image has not been proven to be from the exact block used for molecular assays. The archived specimen records also do not reliably identify frozen versus FFPE preparation, so equivalence to the TCGA diagnostic preparation is not established. These limitations must be retained in the external-validation report. The current stage documents tumor annotations and decode readiness; visual quality review and image-based exclusions are still pending.
27: 
38: The counts are before visual image QC and are not final evaluated sample sizes. Binary labels are 0 = negative and 1 = positive. PAM50 uses the original TCGA model order: 0 = LumA, 1 = LumB, 2 = Basal, 3 = Her2. Normal-like and missing PAM50 are excluded. Missing and equivocal HER2 are excluded; gene amplification, proteomic status and PAM50 are not substituted for clinical HER2.
39: 
40: PIK3CA positivity requires a reported mutation in the original TCGA qualifying variant-class set. The cBioPortal sequenced list, sample `sequenced` flags and WXS profile matrix agree on all 122 samples. A negative reference means no qualifying reported mutation in this profiled denominator, not guaranteed base-level callability or clinically confirmed wild type. The original TCGA class set was checked directly against the supplied v2 labeling script. Source somatic filtering differs across cohorts and remains a limitation.
41: 
42: The endpoint definitions are frozen before external feature extraction or prediction. The future TCGA-trained models and calibrators must also be frozen before inspecting external outcomes. No external-outcome tuning, model selection or recalibration is allowed in the primary analysis.
43: 
48: ## Run the pilot
49: 
50: Use `Start-BRCAExternalStage1.ps1 -Mode Pilot` with your existing BioMedical project and `D:\CPTAC_BRCA_WSI` as the new image directory. The script uses the existing `.venv-brca-revision` environment to decode the downloaded slides. No new Hugging Face login is needed for this acquisition stage.
51: 
52: | Patient | Pilot slide | Size (MB) |
53: | --- | --- | --- |
58: The pilot selects one slide from each of the first three patient identifiers, without inspecting labels. It verifies downloading, TIFF/WSI decoding, dimensions and physical pixel spacing, and generates thumbnails for review. It does not estimate performance. Partial files can be resumed by rerunning the same command. Existing completed files are checked against their receipts rather than overwritten.
59: 
60: At completion, upload the report ZIP printed by the script. It includes acquisition receipts, decode checks and thumbnails, but no full slide images or credentials. If a transfer is interrupted, the script preserves partial files and writes an interruption report where possible.
61: 
62: `-Mode Audit` only checks package integrity and required disk space for the pilot. `-Mode DownloadAll` downloads the full locked inventory; it is provided for the later acquisition step. Start with the pilot so its decoder checks and thumbnails can be reviewed before the full transfer. The original TCGA images, features and results are not changed.
63: 
64: ## Subsequent work
65: 
66: After a successful pilot: review slide quality; complete acquisition; freeze an outcome-independent patch/QC protocol consistent with the TCGA protocol; extract UNI and Hibou-B on the same external patches; finalize and checksum the TCGA-trained prediction objects; and evaluate each endpoint once with patient-level uncertainty. Same-patient links, abbreviated-specimen sensitivity, source assay differences and pretraining uncertainty must accompany the final results. No external accuracy or AUROC is available at stage 1.

FILE: Elicit - TCGA-BRCA institution-aware generalization and calibration experiment plan.md
Line numbers refer to the displayed text (JSON may be reformatted).
36: Use the available tissue-source-site or collection-center proxy to create grouped folds. Prefer leave-one-group-out validation when the number of groups permits; otherwise use grouped five-fold cross-validation with no group shared between train and test. Report the number and size of held-out groups and label prevalence per fold.
37: 
38: If site labels are too sparse or confounded with the endpoint, use a **blocked stress test** instead: hold out the largest identifiable acquisition groups, scanner/stain strata, or slide batches, and report this as a domain-shift test rather than an external validation claim.
39: 
40: ### C. Acquisition perturbation test
41: 
42: Evaluate the frozen model under realistic stain and scanner perturbations: brightness/contrast shifts, color deconvolution perturbations, blur/compression, and resolution changes. These tests should not replace real external data, but they reveal whether uncertainty rises when image appearance moves away from training data. The value of this stress test is supported by recent work showing that foundation-model representation robustness under domain shift can matter more than adding MIL complexity [^6].
43: 
44: ### D. External benchmark, if available
45: 
46: After all TCGA analyses are locked, evaluate once on CPTAC-BRCA or another independent breast cohort with compatible labels. Treat it as external validation only if patient identity, preprocessing, and model-selection separation are documented. CPTAC-BRCA has already been used as an independent cohort for WSI protein-level and response modeling [^4].
47: 
48: ## Model ladder
49: 
105: - **Patient leakage through repeated slides or tiles:** enforce a manifest-level split and unit-test that patient IDs are disjoint.
106: - **Site-label confounding:** report group prevalence and use group-aware folds; do not claim domain robustness if site and endpoint are inseparable.
107: - **Shortcut learning from stain/scanner artifacts:** use acquisition perturbations, stain normalization as a sensitivity analysis, and occlusion tests focused on background regions.
108: - **Class imbalance:** use prevalence-aware metrics, fold balancing only within training data, and avoid reporting accuracy as the primary metric.
109: - **Overconfident predictions:** fit temperature scaling or isotonic regression inside training folds; never calibrate on the final test set.
110: - **Multimodal availability bias:** compare complete-case, WSI-only, and missing-modality routing performance; report who is deferred or excluded.
114: ## Expected deliverable
115: 
116: The result should be a **validation dossier**, not just a leaderboard: a patient-level data manifest, preregistered split definitions, model card, fold-by-fold discrimination and calibration tables, domain-shift plots, missing-modality analysis, explanation-stability panel, and a go/no-go recommendation for external validation. The central output is whether the model's probability estimates remain trustworthy when acquisition context changes—not whether one architecture produces the highest random-split score.
117: 
118: 
119: [^1]: Paul et al., 2024. Survival outcome prediction of breast carcinomas on whole-slide histopathology images using deep learning. Journal of Clinical Oncology.
